Multiplier factors

For
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and
z\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
, let us consider the analytic function on
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
:

J(g,-):z\longrightarrow\sqrt{\det(g)(cz+d)}.

Note that in our choice,
\sqrt{|t|}=|t|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}
, and
\sqrt{e^{i\theta}}=e^{\tfrac{i\theta}{2}}
, for
-\pi<\theta<\pi
, and if
c=0
,
d=-1
,
\sqrt{-1}=-i
in our choice. If
g\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
, then
J(g,i)=\overline{s}(g)^{-1}
.

Lemma 6.19
.

J(g,z)=J(p_{g},z)J(k_{g},z)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.10
,
J(p_{g},z)=\sqrt[4]{c^{2}+d^{2}}
,
J(k_{g},z)=\sqrt{(\det g)(\tfrac{c}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}}z+\tfrac{d}{\sqrt{c^{2}+d^{2}}})}
.
∎

Lemma 6.20
.

J(gp_{z},i)=J(g,z)J(p_{z},i)
.

Proof.

Write
p_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}y&x\\
0&(\det p_{z})y^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

z=yx\det p_{z}+iy^{2}\det p_{z};

J(p_{z},i)=\sqrt{y^{-1}};

J(g,z)=\sqrt{\det g(cz+d)};

gp_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}ay&ax+by^{-1}\det p_{z}\\
cy&cx+dy^{-1}\det p_{z}\end{pmatrix};

\displaystyle J(gp_{z},i)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{(cyi+cx+dy^{-1}\det p_{z})\det(gp_{z})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{y^{-1}\det g(cz+d)}

\displaystyle=J(g,z)J(p_{z},i).

∎

6.16.
Modular forms from an extended Weil representation

Let
\overline{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
denote the two-degree covering group over
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
associated to the above
2
-cocycle
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}
. Go back to
(\ref{Cdou})
, and modify the action of
\Pi_{\psi}(g)
on
L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{R})
by
\overline{s}(g)
. Let us write

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g)=\Pi_{\psi}(g)m_{X^{\ast}}(g).

(6.31)

Then:

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g_{1})\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g_{2})

\displaystyle=\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2}).

Note that
\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}=\overline{\overline{\Pi}}_{\psi}\overline{s}(g)^{-1}
. By (
6.27
)-(
6.30
), the representation
\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}
of
\overline{\operatorname{SL}^{\pm}_{2}}(\mathbb{R})
can be realized on
L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{R})
by the following formulas:

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}[u(b)]f([\epsilon,x])=e^{\pi i\epsilon x^{2}b}f([\epsilon,x]);

(6.32)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}[h(a)]f([\epsilon,x])=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1-\operatorname{sgn}(a)]}|a|^{1/2}(a,\epsilon)_{F}f([\epsilon,xa]);

(6.33)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}[\omega]f([\epsilon,x])=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(\epsilon,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi i\epsilon xy}f([\epsilon,-y])dy;

(6.34)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}[h_{-1},t]f([\epsilon,x])=tf([-\epsilon,x]).

(6.35)

Recall the notations
h,e^{+},e^{-},J^{0},J^{+},J^{-}
from (
6.24
). Write
f([1,x])=f_{1}(x)
and
f([-1,x])=f_{2}(x)
. Assume
f_{i}\in S(\mathbb{R})
. Then:

1) Note that
e^{th}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix}
and
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(e^{t_{1}h},e^{t_{2}h})=1
. So let us consider the one parameter group homomorphism:

\exp:th\longrightarrow[\begin{pmatrix}e^{t}&0\\
0&e^{-t}\end{pmatrix},1].

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(h)f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([e^{th},1])f([\epsilon,x])\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{e^{\tfrac{t}{2}}f([\epsilon,xe^{t}])\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\{\tfrac{1}{2}+x\tfrac{d}{dx}\}f([\epsilon,x]).

2) Note that
e^{te^{+}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
and
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(e^{t_{1}e^{+}},e^{t_{2}e^{+}})=1
. So let us consider the one parameter group homomorphism:

\exp:te^{+}\longrightarrow[\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1].

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{+})f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\Pi_{\psi}([e^{te^{+}},1])f([\epsilon,x])\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{e^{\pi i\epsilon x^{2}t}f([\epsilon,x])\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\pi i\epsilon x^{2}f([\epsilon,x]).

3) Note that
\exp(te^{-})=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
t&1\end{pmatrix}=\omega^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}1&-t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\omega
. Moreover, we have:

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},u(-t))

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},u(-t))

\displaystyle=(x(\omega^{-1}),x(u(-t)))_{\mathbb{R}}(-x(\omega^{-1})x(u(-t)),x(\omega^{-1}u(-t)))_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle=1;

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-t),\omega)

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-t),\omega)

\displaystyle=(x(\omega^{-1}u(-t)),x(\omega))_{\mathbb{R}}(-x(\omega^{-1}u(-t))x(\omega),x(u_{-}(t))_{\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle=1;

\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1},\omega)=1;

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(t_{1}),u_{-}(t_{2}))

\displaystyle=\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(u_{-}(t_{1}),u_{-}(t_{2}))

(6.36)

\displaystyle=(x(u_{-}(t_{1})),x(u_{-}(t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}(-x(u_{-}(t_{1}))x(u_{-}(t_{2})),x(u_{-}(t_{1}+t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}};

(6.37)

•

If
t_{1}t_{2}=0
,
(\ref{qqq})=1
.

•

If
t_{1}t_{2}>0
, assume
t_{1}>0
and
t_{2}<0
; then
(x(u_{-}(t_{1})),x(u_{-}(t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=1
,
(-x(u_{-}(t_{1}))x(u_{-}(t_{2})),x(u_{-}(t_{1}+t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=(-t_{1}t_{2},x(u_{-}(t_{1}+t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=1
. So
(\ref{qqq})=1
.

•

If
t_{1}>0
,
t_{2}>0
,
(x(u_{-}(t_{1})),x(u_{-}(t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=(t_{1},t_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}=1
,
(-x(u_{-}(t_{1}))x(u_{-}(t_{2})),x(u_{-}(t_{1}+t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=(-t_{1}t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}=1
, so
(\ref{qqq})=1
.

•

If
t_{1}<0
,
t_{2}<0
,
(x(u_{-}(t_{1})),x(u_{-}(t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=(t_{1},t_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}=-1
,
(-x(u_{-}(t_{1}))x(u_{-}(t_{2})),x(u_{-}(t_{1}+t_{2}))_{\mathbb{R}}=(-t_{1}t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2})_{\mathbb{R}}=-1
. Therefore,
(\ref{qqq})=1
.

Let us consider the one parameter group homomorphism:

\exp:te^{-}\longrightarrow[\omega^{-1},1][\begin{pmatrix}1&-t\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},1][\omega,1]=[e^{te^{-}},1].

a)

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\exp(te^{-}))f_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([-\omega,1])\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([u(-t),1])\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{1}\}(x)

\displaystyle=ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi ixy}\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([u(-t),1])\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{1}\}(y)dy

\displaystyle=ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi ixy}e^{-\pi iy^{2}t}\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{1}\}(y)dy

\displaystyle=ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}[e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}]\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi ixy}e^{-\pi iy^{2}t}\widehat{f_{1}}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\pi ity^{2}}\widehat{f_{1}}(y)e^{2\pi ixy}dy.

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})f([1,x])

\displaystyle=d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})f_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\exp(te^{-}))f_{1}(x)\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(-\pi iy^{2})\widehat{f_{1}}(y)e^{2\pi ixy}dy

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{f_{1}}(y)e^{2\pi ixy}dy

.

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}f_{1}(x).

b)

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f([-1,x])

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}f([-1,-y])dy

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}f_{2}(-y)dy

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\widehat{f_{2}}(-x).

[-\omega,1]=[\omega,1][h(-1),1]

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([-\omega,1])f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([-\omega,1])f([-1,x])

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}[\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([h(-1),1])f]([-1,-y])dy

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}f([-1,y])dy

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}f_{2}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\widehat{f_{2}}(x).

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([u(-t),1])f([-1,x])=e^{\pi ix^{2}t}f([-1,x])

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\exp(te^{-}))f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([-\omega,1])\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([u(-t),1])\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{2}\}(x)

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([u(-t),1])\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{2}\}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}e^{\pi iy^{2}t}\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([\omega,1])f_{2}\}(y)dy

\displaystyle=-ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)[e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\nu(-1,\omega)]\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}e^{\pi iy^{2}t}\widehat{f_{2}}(-y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}\widehat{f_{2}}(-y)e^{\pi iy^{2}t}dy.

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})f([-1,x])

\displaystyle=d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\exp(te^{-}))f_{2}(x)\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\pi ixy}\widehat{f_{2}}(-y)e^{\pi iy^{2}t}dy\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{2\pi ixy}\widehat{f_{2}}(y)e^{\pi iy^{2}}dy\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\pi iy^{2})\widehat{f_{2}}(y)e^{2\pi ixy}dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi i}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{f_{2}}(y)e^{2\pi ixy}dy

.

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\pi i}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}f_{2}(x).

Hence:

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})f([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\Pi_{\psi}(\exp(te^{-}))f([\epsilon,x])\Big\}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon i}{4\pi}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}f([\epsilon,x]).

Analogue of the lemma in
[
LiVe
, p.199]
, we have:

Lemma 6.21
.

Let
A([\epsilon,x])=e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}
,
B([\epsilon,x])=xe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}\in L^{2}(\mu_{2}\times\mathbb{R})
. Then:

(1)

[d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{0})]A([\epsilon,x])=\frac{i}{2}A([\epsilon,x])
and
[d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{-})]A([\epsilon,x])=0
,

(2)

[d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{0})]B([\epsilon,x])=\frac{3i}{2}B([\epsilon,x])
and
[d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{-})]B([\epsilon,x])=0
.

Proof.

1)

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{+})A([\epsilon,x])-d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})A([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\pi i\epsilon x^{2}A([\epsilon,x])-\frac{\epsilon i}{4\pi}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}A([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\pi i\epsilon x^{2}A([\epsilon,x])-\pi i\epsilon x^{2}A([\epsilon,x])-\frac{\epsilon i}{4\pi}[-2\epsilon\pi e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{i}{2}A([\epsilon,x]);

\displaystyle 2d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{-})A([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=id\Pi_{\psi}(h)A([\epsilon,x])+d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{+})A([\epsilon,x])+d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})A([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{i}{2}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-2\epsilon\pi ix^{2}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+\pi i\epsilon x^{2}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+\pi i\epsilon x^{2}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-\tfrac{i}{2}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}

\displaystyle=0.

2)

\displaystyle d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{+})B([\epsilon,x])-d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})B([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\pi i\epsilon x^{2}B([\epsilon,x])-\frac{\epsilon i}{4\pi}\frac{d^{2}}{dx^{2}}B([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\pi i\epsilon x^{3}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-\frac{\epsilon i}{4\pi}[-2\pi\epsilon xe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-4\pi\epsilon xe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+4\pi^{2}x^{3}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{3i}{2}B([\epsilon,x]);

\displaystyle 2d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{-})B([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=id\overline{\Pi}(h)B([\epsilon,x])+d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{+})B([\epsilon,x])+d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(e^{-})B([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=\tfrac{i}{2}xe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+ixe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-2\pi i\epsilon x^{3}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+\pi i\epsilon x^{3}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}+\pi i\epsilon x^{3}e^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}-\tfrac{3i}{2}xe^{-\epsilon\pi x^{2}}

\displaystyle=0.

∎

As a consequence(cf.
[
Kn
, Chapter §3]
), we have:

Lemma 6.22
.

(1)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t},e^{\tfrac{-it}{2}}])A([\epsilon,x])=A([\epsilon,x])
,

(2)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t},e^{\tfrac{-3it}{2}}])B([\epsilon,x])=B([\epsilon,x])
,

for
g_{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos t&\sin t\\
-\sin t&\cos t\end{pmatrix}
, for
-\pi<t<\pi
.

Proof.

1)

\lim\limits_{t\to 0}\frac{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t},e^{\tfrac{-it}{2}}])A([\epsilon,x])-A([\epsilon,x])}{t}=d\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(J^{-})A([\epsilon,x])-\tfrac{i}{2}A([\epsilon,x])=0.

Consequently,

\lim\limits_{t\to 0}\frac{\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t},e^{\tfrac{-it}{2}}])\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t_{0}},e^{\tfrac{-it_{0}}{2}}])A([\epsilon,x])-\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([g_{t_{0}},e^{\tfrac{-it_{0}}{2}}])A([\epsilon,x])}{t}=0.

2) Similarly.
∎

Lemma 6.23
.

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([h(-1),-i])_{\mathbb{R}}A[\epsilon,x]=(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}A[\epsilon,x]
and
\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([h(-1),(-i)^{3}])B[\epsilon,x]=(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}B[\epsilon,x]
.

Proof.

By (
6.33
), we have:

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([h(-1),-i])A([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=(-i)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1+1]}(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}A([\epsilon,-x])

\displaystyle=(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}A([\epsilon,-x]).

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}([h(-1),i])B([\epsilon,x])

\displaystyle=ie^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1+1]}(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}B([\epsilon,-x])

\displaystyle=(-1,-\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}B([\epsilon,-x])

\displaystyle=(-1,\epsilon)_{\mathbb{R}}B([\epsilon,x]).

∎

6.16.1.
Theta series

For
p=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
0&a^{-1}\det p\end{pmatrix}\in P_{>0}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
, let us write

p=\begin{pmatrix}a&0\\
0&a^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\tfrac{b\det p}{a}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\det p\end{pmatrix}.

By (
6.20
),
g
corresponds to the element
z_{p}=ab\det p+ia^{2}\det p
of
\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
. By (
4.4
)(
4.5
), we have:

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p)A([\epsilon,x])=a^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e^{-(\det p)\epsilon\pi x^{2}a^{2}}\cdot e^{\pi i\epsilon(\det p)x^{2}ab}=a^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e^{i\epsilon\pi x^{2}z_{p}}.

(6.38)

\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p)B([\epsilon,x])=a^{\tfrac{3}{2}}xe^{-(\det p)\epsilon\pi x^{2}a^{2}}\cdot e^{\pi i\epsilon(\det p)x^{2}ab}=a^{\tfrac{3}{2}}xe^{i\epsilon\pi x^{2}z_{p}}.

(6.39)

Let us consider
\Gamma=\breve{\Gamma}(2)^{\pm}
, which is a discrete group of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
. Recall
\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}
from (
6.11
).
Let us write
g=p_{g}k_{g}
, for
g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
,
p_{g}\in P_{>0}^{\pm}(\mathbb{R})
,
k_{g}\in\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Then:

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(A)([\epsilon,0])=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}A(\epsilon,l)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}A(\epsilon,n).

z_{p_{g}}=\frac{ai+b}{ci+d}=gi=z_{g}.

If
k_{g}\neq h(-1)
, then

J(g,i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g)A=J(p_{g},i)J(k_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(k_{g})A=J(p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})A,

J(g,i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(g)B=J(p_{g},i)^{3}J(k_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(k_{g})^{3}B=J(p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})B.

Let:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}(p_{g},\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J(p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})A(\epsilon,n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\epsilon\pi n^{2}z_{g}}.

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}(p_{g},\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}J(p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})B(\epsilon,n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\epsilon\pi n^{2}z_{g}}.

Let:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}(z,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i\epsilon\pi n^{2}z},

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}(z,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i\epsilon\pi n^{2}z},

for
z\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
,
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
. Let us consider the explicit action of
\Gamma
on
\theta_{1/2}(z,\epsilon)
and
\theta_{3/2}(z,\epsilon)
from Section
6.7
. Note that if
k_{\gamma p_{g}}=h(-1)
, then
\gamma p_{g}=-p_{\gamma p_{g}}
, which implies that
-\gamma\in P^{\pm}_{>0}(\mathbb{R})
, and then
\gamma=h(-1)
or
h(-1)h_{-1}
. For
\gamma\in\Gamma
, we have:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}(\gamma p_{g},\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(\gamma p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\gamma p_{g})A\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,p_{g}i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\gamma)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,p_{g}i)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)\Pi_{\psi}(\gamma)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{g}i=z}}{{=}}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,z)\Upsilon(\gamma)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)J(\gamma,z)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z};

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}(\gamma p_{g},\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(\gamma p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\gamma p_{g})B\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,p_{g}i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(\gamma)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,p_{g}i)^{3}m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)\Pi_{\psi}(\gamma)\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J(p_{g},i)^{3}\overline{\Pi}_{\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)[\epsilon,0]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{g}i=z}}{{=}}\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}J(\gamma,z)^{3}\Upsilon(\gamma)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)J(\gamma,z)^{3}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z}.

for the constant
\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}\Upsilon(\gamma)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)
, where
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}
only depends on
\gamma
and
\det g
.

6.16.1.1

Let us give the expression of
\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma)
in the following. Let
\gamma=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma
. Let us define
\operatorname{sgn}(z)=1
or
-1
according to
z\in\mathcal{H}
or
\mathcal{H}^{-}
.

•

If
c=0
,
\Upsilon(\gamma)=(\epsilon\det\gamma,a)_{\mathbb{R}}
,
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}=(\det p_{g},a)_{\mathbb{R}}=(\operatorname{sgn}z_{g},a)_{\mathbb{R}}
,
m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1-\operatorname{sgn}(a)]}
.

\displaystyle\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}\Upsilon(\gamma)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1-\operatorname{sgn}(a)]}

(6.40)

\displaystyle=(\epsilon\det\gamma,a)_{\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{sgn}z_{g},a)_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[1-\operatorname{sgn}(a)]}.

(6.41)

•

If
c\neq 0
,
\Upsilon(\gamma)=e^{\tfrac{\pi i(1-\epsilon)}{4}}(c\det\gamma,\epsilon\det\gamma)_{\mathbb{R}}\beta(d,(\det\gamma)\epsilon c)^{-1}
,
\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})=1
,
m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[-\operatorname{sgn}(c)]}
.

\displaystyle\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\overline{C}_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma,p_{g})^{-1}\Upsilon(\gamma)m_{X^{\ast}}(\gamma)

(6.42)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}[-\operatorname{sgn}(c)]}e^{\tfrac{\pi i(1-\epsilon)}{4}}(c\det\gamma,\epsilon\det\gamma)_{\mathbb{R}}\beta(d,(\det\gamma)\epsilon c)^{-1}.

(6.43)

Remark 6.24
.

If
\epsilon=1
,
\det\gamma=1
, the above constant
\lambda^{\pm}
is compatible with the constant
\lambda
given in
[
LiVe
, p.2.4.13, theorem]
by adding the factor
J(\gamma,z)
in our choice.

Hence:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}(\tfrac{az+b}{cz+d},\epsilon)=\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)\sqrt{\det\gamma(cz+d)}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z},

(6.44)

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}(\tfrac{az+b}{cz+d},\epsilon)=\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)(\sqrt{\det\gamma(cz+d)})^{3}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}ne^{i(\det\gamma)\epsilon\pi n^{2}z},

(6.45)

for
z\in\mathcal{H}^{\pm}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma
if
\epsilon=1
or
\gamma\in\Gamma\setminus\{h(-1),h(-1)h_{-1}\}
if
\epsilon\neq-1
, and some constant
\lambda^{\pm}(\gamma,\epsilon)
.

Remark 6.25
.

One can add
\{h(-1),h(-1)h_{-1}\}
to change the constant in above two statements, but it is indeed a projective representation of
\Gamma
, which does not factor through the group
\Gamma/\{\pm 1\}
.

7.
Appendix: Weil representation of
\operatorname{Mp}_{2}(\mathbb{R})

The purpose of this section is to study the works
[
LiVe
]
,
[
Pe
]
, and
[
Rao
]
in the simple case—
\dim W=2
. Let
\psi=\psi_{0}:t\longrightarrow e^{2\pi it}
. Let
dx
denote the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
. Recall the classical Fourier transformation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}:

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{R});

\displaystyle f\longmapsto\mathcal{F}(f)(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-xt)f(t)dt

for
f\in S(\mathbb{R})
. By convection, let us write
\hat{f}=\mathcal{F}(f)
. Then:

•

\hat{\hat{f}}(x)=f(-x)
.

•

\hat{f}=f
, for
f(x)=e^{-\pi x^{2}}
.

•

(\tau_{t}(f))^{\textasciicircum}(x)=\hat{f}(x)e^{2\pi ixt}
, where
\tau_{t}(f)(x)=f(x+t)
.

•

If
g(x)=f(x)e^{2\pi ixt}
, then
\hat{g}(x)=\hat{f}(x-t)
.

•

If
g(x)=\lambda^{-1}f(\lambda^{-1}x)
, then
\hat{g}(x)=\hat{f}(\lambda x)
.

•

\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\hat{f}(x)e^{\pi ix^{2}}dx=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}f(x)e^{-\pi ix^{2}}dx
.(cf.
[
LiVe
, p.50, Lemma]
)

•

\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\hat{f}(x)e^{-\pi ix^{2}}dx=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}f(x)e^{\pi ix^{2}}dx
.(cf.
[
LiVe
, p.50, Lemma]
)

Let
X=\mathbb{R}e_{1}\simeq\mathbb{R}
,
X^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{1}^{\ast}\simeq\mathbb{R}
. By the fixed basis
\{e_{1},e_{1}^{\ast}\}
, let us identity
X
with
\mathbb{R}
, and
X^{\ast}
with
\mathbb{R}
. Let
dx
,
dx^{\ast}
denote the Lebesgue measures on
X
and
X^{\ast}
respectively. Let us give two classical Fourier transformations(cf.
[
We
, p.147]
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{XX^{\ast}}:

\displaystyle L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X^{\ast});

\displaystyle f\longmapsto\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{XX^{\ast}}(f)(x^{\ast})=\int_{X}\psi(\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle)f(x)dx,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}:

\displaystyle L^{2}(X^{\ast})\longrightarrow L^{2}(X);

\displaystyle g\longmapsto\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}(g)(x)=\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(\langle x^{\ast},x\rangle)g(x^{\ast})dx^{\ast},

for
f\in L^{2}(X)
,
g\in L^{2}(X^{\ast})
. Then
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}=\mathcal{F}_{XX^{\ast}}^{{}^{\prime}-1}
. Let
\pi_{\psi}
be the Weil representation of
\operatorname{H}(W)
associated to
\psi
. Then
\pi_{\psi}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{X\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\psi)
. Following
[
Pe
]
, let
\mathcal{H}(X)
be the set of measurable functions
f:\operatorname{H}(W)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that

(i)

f([x,t]h)=\psi(t)f(h)
,
x\in X
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
h\in\operatorname{H}(W)
;

(ii)

\int_{(X\times\mathbb{R})\setminus\operatorname{H}(W)}||f(\dot{h})||^{2}d\dot{h}<+\infty
, where
d\dot{h}
is an
\operatorname{H}(W)
-right invariant measure on
(X\times\mathbb{R})\setminus\operatorname{H}(W)
.

Note that
(X\times\mathbb{R})\setminus\operatorname{H}(W)
is homeomorphic to
X^{\ast}
, so we choose the
d\dot{w}
corresponding to the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}e_{1}^{\ast}
through the base
e_{1}^{\ast}
. Then
\pi_{\psi}
can be realized on
L^{2}(X^{\ast})
by the following formulas:

\pi_{\psi}[(x^{\ast},0)+(x,0)+(0,k)]g(y^{\ast})=\psi(k+\langle x^{\ast}+y^{\ast},x\rangle)g(x^{\ast}+y^{\ast}),

(7.1)

for
g\in L^{2}(X^{\ast})
,
x\in X
,
x^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}
,
k\in\mathbb{R}
. Similarly, we can also consider
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})=\operatorname{Ind}_{X^{\ast}\times F}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\psi)
. By
[
Pe
, p.373]
, we can define the Fourier transformation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast});

\displaystyle f\longmapsto\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}(f)(h)=\int_{X^{\ast}}f([x^{\ast},0]h)dx^{\ast}.

It can be checked that
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}(f)([x^{\ast},t]h)=\psi(t)\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}(f)(h)
, for
[x^{\ast},t]\in X^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, there exists the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}\mathcal{H}(X)@>{\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}}>{}>\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\\
@V{\wr}V{}V@V{\wr}V{}V\\
L^{2}(X^{\ast})@>{\mathcal{G}_{X^{\ast}X}}>{}>L^{2}(X)\end{CD}

Let us check that
\mathcal{G}_{X^{\ast}X}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{X^{\ast}X}(f)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}}f([y^{\ast},0][x,0])dy^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\int_{X^{\ast}}\psi(\langle y^{\ast},x\rangle)f(y^{\ast})dy^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}(f)(x).

Hence
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}=\mathcal{F}_{XX^{\ast}}^{-1}
. For simplicity, we will not distinguish between
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}X}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}X}
. For two general Lagrangian lines
Y^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{Y^{\ast}}
and
Z^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{Z^{\ast}}
, we have:

Y^{\ast}\cap Z^{\ast}\neq 0\textrm{ iff }Y^{\ast}=Z^{\ast}.

1) If
Y^{\ast}\cap Z^{\ast}=0
,
W=Y^{\ast}\oplus Z^{\ast}
. Let us define
A_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}=\langle e_{Y^{\ast}},e_{Z^{\ast}}\rangle
by following
[
Pe
]
. Let
e^{\prime}_{Y^{\ast}}=|A_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{Y^{\ast}}
and
e^{\prime}_{Z^{\ast}}=|A_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{Z^{\ast}}
. Then
\langle e^{\prime}_{Y^{\ast}},e^{\prime}_{Z^{\ast}}\rangle=\pm 1
. By the basis
\{e^{\prime}_{Y^{\ast}},e^{\prime}_{Z^{\ast}}\}
, let
d^{\prime}y^{\ast}
,
d^{\prime}z^{\ast}
denote the Lebesgue measures on
Y^{\ast}
and
Z^{\ast}
respectively. By the basis
\{e_{Y^{\ast}},e_{Z^{\ast}}\}
, let
dy^{\ast}
,
dz^{\ast}
denote the Lebesgue measures on
Y^{\ast}
and
Z^{\ast}
respectively. Similarly, we can define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(Y^{\ast})\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast});

(7.2)

\displaystyle f\longmapsto\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}(f)(h)=\int_{Z^{\ast}}f([z^{\ast},0]h)d^{\prime}z^{\ast}

(7.3)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\qquad\quad\qquad\quad=\int_{Z^{\ast}}f([z^{\ast},0]h)|A_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dz^{\ast}.

(7.4)

2) If
Y^{\ast}\cap Z^{\ast}\neq 0
, then
Y^{\ast}=Z^{\ast}
. In this case, let us define:

\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}.

For
w=xe_{1}+ye_{1}^{\ast}\in W
, let us write
w=(x,y)\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}
. For
g\in\operatorname{Sp}(W)
, let us write
\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}g=A_{g}\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}
, for some
A_{g}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. In this way, we will identity
\operatorname{Sp}(W)
with
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Let us consider two elements
g_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
and
g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}p&q\\
r&s\end{pmatrix}
of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
. Let
e_{Y}=ae_{1}+be_{1}^{\ast}
,
e_{Y^{\ast}}=ce_{1}+de_{1}^{\ast}=e_{1}^{\ast}g_{1}
,
e_{Z}=pe_{1}+qe_{1}^{\ast}
,
e_{Z^{\ast}}=re_{1}+se_{1}^{\ast}=e_{1}^{\ast}g_{2}
. Let
Y=\mathbb{R}e_{Y}
,
Y^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{Y^{\ast}}
,
Z=\mathbb{R}e_{Z}
,
Z^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{Z^{\ast}}
. Following
[
LiVe
, pp.71-73]
, let us first consider the case that
c>0
,
d=0
and
r=1,s>0
. In this case,
Y^{\ast}=X
, and
e_{Y^{\ast}}=ce_{1}
,
Z^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}(e_{1}+se_{1}^{\ast})
. Let us fix
W=X\oplus X^{\ast}
.

a)
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(X)\simeq L^{2}(X^{\ast})
,
|A_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|=c
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\mathcal{F}_{XX^{\ast}}:L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X^{\ast})
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\int_{tce_{1}\in X}f([tce_{1},0][xe^{\ast}_{1},0])|A_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.5)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(tcx)f([tce_{1},0])c^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(tx)f([te_{1},0])c^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.7)

b)
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(X)\simeq L^{2}(X^{\ast})
,
|A_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}|=|cs|=cs
,
\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}=\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X}:L^{2}(X^{\ast})\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([te_{Z^{\ast}},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.8)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([(t+x)e_{1},-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}-tsx][tse^{\ast}_{1},0])(cs)^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.9)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-tsx-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([tse_{1}^{\ast},0])(cs)^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{1}^{\ast},0])c^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.11)

c)
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
|A_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|=1
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}:L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([te_{1}^{\ast},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.12)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([te_{1}+tse_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}][(x-t)e_{1},0])sdt

(7.13)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([(x-t)e_{1},0])sdt

(7.14)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([(x-s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e_{1},0])s^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.15)

d)
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}:L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([te_{Z^{\ast}},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.16)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([(t+x)e_{1}+tse_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{tsx}{2}])dt

(7.17)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([tse_{1}^{\ast},\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}][(t+x)e_{1},0])dt

(7.18)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([(x+t)e_{1},0])dt

(7.19)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([(x+ts^{-\tfrac{1}{2}})e_{1},0])s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.20)

Lemma 7.1

(
[
LiVe
, p.47]
)
.

For the above
X^{\ast},Y^{\ast},Z^{\ast}
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})}
.

Proof.

By the base
e_{1}
, we will identity
X
with
\mathbb{R}
. For
f\in S(X)
, let
g(t)=f(t)e^{-2\pi ixts}
,
h(t)=s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}g(s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)
. Then:

\hat{h}(t)=\hat{g}(s^{1/2}t)=\hat{f}(s^{1/2}t+sx).

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

(7.21)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)([ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{1}^{\ast},0])c^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.22)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}u-ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([ue_{1},0])dudt

(7.23)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}u+ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([ue_{1},0])dudt

(7.24)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}})dt

(7.25)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{(t-s^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x)^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}})dt

(7.26)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(ts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}+sx)dt

(7.27)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{h}(t)dt

(7.28)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}h(t)e^{\pi it^{2}}dt

(7.29)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e^{\pi isx^{2}}e^{-2\pi ixts^{\tfrac{1}{2}}}e^{\pi it^{2}}dt

(7.30)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e^{\pi i(t-s^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x)^{2}}dt

(7.31)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(s^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t+x)\psi(\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})dt

(7.32)

\displaystyle=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1}).

(7.33)

∎

Let us consider the second case that
c>0
,
d=0
and
r=1,s=-|s|<0
. In this case,
Y^{\ast}=X=\mathbb{R}ce_{1}
,
Z^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}(e_{1}+se_{1}^{\ast})
. Let us fix
W=X\oplus X^{\ast}
.

b’)
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(X)\simeq L^{2}(X^{\ast})
,
\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}=\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X}:L^{2}(X^{\ast})\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([te_{Z^{\ast}},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.34)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([(t+x)e_{1},-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}-tsx][tse^{\ast}_{1},0])(c|s|)^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.35)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-tsx-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([tse_{1}^{\ast},0])(c|s|)^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.36)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x+\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([-t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{1}^{\ast},0])c^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.37)

c’)
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}:L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([te_{1}^{\ast},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.38)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([xe_{1}+te_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{tx}{2}])dt

(7.39)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([xe_{1}+tse_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{tsx}{2}])sdt

(7.40)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{1}^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}}f([te_{1}+tse_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}][(x-t)e_{1},0])sdt

(7.41)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([(x-t)e_{1},0])sdt

(7.42)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([(x+|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e_{1},0])|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.43)

d’)
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{H}(Z^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(X)
,
\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}:L^{2}(X)\longrightarrow L^{2}(X)
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([te_{Z^{\ast}},0][xe_{1},0])|A_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.44)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([(t+x)e_{1}+tse_{1}^{\ast},-\tfrac{tsx}{2}])dt

(7.45)

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Z^{\ast}}\in Z^{\ast}}f([tse_{1}^{\ast},\tfrac{st^{2}}{2}][(t+x)e_{1},0])dt

(7.46)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{st^{2}}{2})f([(x+t)e_{1},0])dt

(7.47)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})f([(x+t|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}})e_{1},0])|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}dt.

(7.48)

Lemma 7.2

(
[
LiVe
, pp.47-48]
)
.

For the above
X^{\ast},Y^{\ast},Z^{\ast}
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})}
.

Proof.

By the base
e_{1}
, we will identity
X
with
\mathbb{R}
. For
f\in S(X)
, let
g(t)=f(t)e^{-2\pi ixts}
,
h(t)=|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}g(|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)
. Then:

\hat{h}(t)=\hat{g}(|s|^{1/2}t)=\hat{f}(|s|^{1/2}t+sx).

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x+\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)([-t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}e_{1}^{\ast},0])c^{\tfrac{1}{2}}dt

(7.49)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x+\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}})dt

(7.50)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{(t+|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x)^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}})dt

(7.51)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{f}(t|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}+sx)dt

(7.52)

\displaystyle=\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})\hat{h}(t)dt

(7.53)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\psi(\tfrac{sx^{2}}{2})\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}h(t)e^{-\pi it^{2}}dt

(7.54)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e^{\pi isx^{2}}e^{2\pi ixt|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}}e^{-\pi it^{2}}dt

(7.55)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t)e^{-\pi i(t-|s|^{\tfrac{1}{2}}x)^{2}}dt

(7.56)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}f(|s|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}t+x)\psi(-\tfrac{t^{2}}{2})dt

(7.57)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}(f)(xe_{1}).

(7.58)

∎

Lemma 7.3

(
[
LiVe
, p.56, Theorem]
)
.

\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})}
.

Proof.

1) If
c=0
, then
Y^{\ast}=X^{\ast}
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}
. On the right hand side,
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})=1
, so the result holds.

2) If
r=0
, then
Z^{\ast}=X^{\ast}
,
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}=\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}
,
\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}
. Moreover,
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})=1
, so the result holds.

3) Note that
g_{2}g_{1}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
-cs+dr&\ast\end{pmatrix}
. If
cs-dr=0
, then
Y^{\ast}=Z^{\ast}
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=\operatorname{Id}
. On the other hand,
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})=1
.

4) Assume
cr(cs-dr)\neq 0
. Then
Z^{\ast}\neq X^{\ast}\neq Y^{\ast}
, and
Y^{\ast}\neq Z^{\ast}
. Note that

\begin{pmatrix}c&d\\
r&s\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{c}{r}&0\\
1&\tfrac{r}{c}(cs-rd)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}r&\tfrac{d}{c}r\\
0&r^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Hence there exists
p=\begin{pmatrix}x&y\\
0&x^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{R})
such that

(c,d)p=(c^{\prime},0),\quad\quad(r,s)p=(1,s^{\prime})

for some positive
c^{\prime}
, and non-zero
s^{\prime}
. Let
\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}^{\prime}\\
e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\end{pmatrix}=p\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}
. Under the basis of
\{e_{1}^{\prime},e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\}
,

e_{Y^{\ast}}=(c,d)\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}=(c,d)p\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}^{\prime}\\
e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\end{pmatrix}=(c^{\prime},0)\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}^{\prime}\\
e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\end{pmatrix},

e_{Y^{\ast}}=(r,s)\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{1}^{\ast}\end{pmatrix}=(r,s)p\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}^{\prime}\\
e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\end{pmatrix}=(1,s^{\prime})\begin{pmatrix}e_{1}^{\prime}\\
e_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\ast}\end{pmatrix}.

By Lemmas
7.1
,
7.2
, we have:

\mathcal{F}_{X^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Z^{\ast}Y^{\ast}}\circ\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(-s^{\prime})}{4}}\operatorname{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})}.

In this case,

g_{1}p=\begin{pmatrix}\ast&-c^{{}^{\prime}-1}\\
c^{\prime}&0\end{pmatrix},\quad g_{2}p=\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
1&s^{\prime}\end{pmatrix},\quad g_{2}g_{1}^{-1}=g_{2}p(g_{1}p)^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\ast&\ast\\
-c^{\prime}s^{\prime}&\ast\end{pmatrix}.

Hence
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}p(g_{1}p)^{-1},g_{1}p)=e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c^{\prime}1(-c^{\prime})s^{\prime})}{4}}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(-s^{\prime})}{4}}
. So the result is right.
∎

7.1.
Lattice model

Let
L=\mathbb{Z}e_{1}+\mathbb{Z}e_{1}^{\ast}
be a self-dual lattice with respect to
\psi
. Then
\pi_{\psi}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\psi
. By Example
2.4
, we let
\mathcal{H}(L)
be the set of measurable functions
f:W\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that

(i)

f(l+w)=\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2}-\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2})f(w)
, for all
l=x_{l}+x_{l}^{\ast}\in L=(X\cap L)\oplus(X^{\ast}\cap L)
,
w\in W
;

(ii)

\int_{L\setminus W}||f(\dot{w})||^{2}d\dot{w}<+\infty
.

Here, we choose a
W
-right invariant measure
d\dot{w}
on
L\setminus W
. By the basis
\{e_{1},e_{1}^{\ast}\}
,
L\setminus W\simeq(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})^{2}
, so we choose the measure
d\dot{w}
, which corresponds to the product normal Haar measure on
(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})^{2}
. Following
[
LiVe
, p.149]
, let
Y^{\ast}
be a Lagrangian plane of
W
such that
Y^{\ast}\cap L\neq 0
. Then there exists two coprime integers
c,d
such that
ce_{1}+de_{1}^{\ast}\in Y^{\ast}
. Let us choose two integers
a,b
such that
ad-bc=1
, i.e.
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let
e_{Y}=ae_{1}+be_{1}^{\ast}
,
e_{Y^{\ast}}=ce_{1}+de_{1}^{\ast}
,
Y=\mathbb{R}e_{Y}
. So
W=Y\oplus Y^{\ast}
and
L=\mathbb{Z}e_{Y}\oplus\mathbb{Z}e_{Y^{\ast}}
.

Let us consider
2\mid cd
. By
[
We
, pp.164-165]
, or
[
LiVe
, pp.142-145]
, there exists a pair of explicit isomorphisms between
\mathcal{H}(Y^{\ast})\simeq L^{2}(Y)
and
\mathcal{H}(L)
, given as follows:

\theta_{L,Y^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})(w)=\sum_{l\in L/L\cap Y^{\ast}}f^{\prime}(w+l)\psi(\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2}+\tfrac{\langle y_{l},y^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2}),

(7.59)

\theta_{Y^{\ast},L}(f)(y)=\int_{Y^{\ast}/Y^{\ast}\cap L}f([\dot{y}^{\ast},0]+[y,0])d\dot{y}^{\ast},

(7.60)

for
w=x+x^{\ast}\in W
,
y\in Y
,
f^{\prime}\in S(Y)\subseteq L^{2}(Y)
,
f\in L^{1}(W)\cap\mathcal{H}(L)
. It can be checked that
\theta_{L,Y^{\ast}}
and
\theta_{Y^{\ast},L}
define a pair of inverse intertwining operators between
\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{H}(L)}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\psi
and
\operatorname{Ind}_{Y^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}}^{\operatorname{H}(W)}\psi
.

Let us consider
g_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}p&q\\
r&s\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let
e_{Z}=pe_{1}+qe_{1}^{\ast}
,
e_{Z^{\ast}}=re_{1}+se_{1}^{\ast}=e_{1}^{\ast}g_{2}
. Let
Z=\mathbb{R}e_{Z}
,
Z^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}e_{Z^{\ast}}
. Following
[
LiVe
, p.149]
, let
\beta(Y^{\ast},Z^{\ast})
be an element in
T
such that

\theta_{Y^{\ast},L}\circ\theta_{L,Z^{\ast}}=\beta(Y^{\ast},Z^{\ast})\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast}Z^{\ast}}.

By Lemma
7.3
, we have:

\beta(X^{\ast},Z^{\ast})\beta(Z^{\ast},Y^{\ast})\beta(Y^{\ast},X^{\ast})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})^{-1}.

Let us define

\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1})=\beta(X^{\ast}g_{1},X^{\ast}).

Lemma 7.4
.

\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(h_{1},h_{2})=\widetilde{\beta}(h_{1})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(h_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(h_{1}h_{2})
, for
h_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{i}&\beta_{i}\\
c_{i}&d_{i}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
2\mid c_{i}d_{i}
.

Proof.

\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2})^{-1}=\beta(X^{\ast},Z^{\ast})=\widetilde{\beta}([g_{2}g_{1}^{-1}]g_{1})^{-1}
,

\beta(Z^{\ast},Y^{\ast})=\beta(X^{\ast}g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},X^{\ast})=\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1})
,
\beta(Y^{\ast},X^{\ast})=\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1})
. Hence
\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2}^{-1}g_{1})\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2}g_{1}^{-1},g_{1})^{-1}
. Then write
h_{1}=g_{2}g_{1}^{-1}
,
h_{2}=g_{1}
,
h_{1}h_{2}=g_{2}
.
∎

Following
[
LiVe
]
, let
\breve{\Gamma}(2)
be the group generated by
\Gamma(2)
and
\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
. It is clear that
\breve{\Gamma}(2)\simeq\langle\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\rangle\ltimes\Gamma(2)
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The restriction of
[\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}]
on
\breve{\Gamma}(2)
is trivial, with an explicit trivialization:

\widetilde{\beta}:\breve{\Gamma}(2)\longrightarrow\mu_{8};g\longmapsto\widetilde{\beta}(g),

such that
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1}g_{2})
, for
g_{i}\in\breve{\Gamma}(2)
.

Proof.

It following from the above lemma.
∎

7.2.
The expression of
\widetilde{\beta}(g)

Let
c,d
be two coprime integers with
cd\neq 0
. Following
[
LiVe
, p.162]
, let us define

\betaup(c,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}|d|^{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{|d|-1}e^{-\tfrac{\pi icn^{2}}{d}}.

Lemma 7.6
.

If
cd
is even,
\beta(d,c)\beta(c,d)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(dc)}
.

Proof.

See
[
LiVe
, p.170, Prop.]
.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

If
d>0
,
c=2c^{\prime}
, then

\betaup(d,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4),\\
(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

If
d<0
,
c=2c^{\prime}
, then

\betaup(d,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(cd)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4),\\
(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})(-i)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(cd)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Assume
d>0
. By the above lemma,
\betaup(c,d)\betaup(d,c)=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(cd)}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}
. Moreover, by
[
BeEvWi
, Thm. 1.5.2]
, we have:

\betaup(c,d)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{d})&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4),\\
-(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{d})i&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

∎

Let us calculate the constant
\widetilde{\beta}(g)
, for
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
2\mid cd
. In this case,
X^{\ast}g=Y^{\ast}=\mathbb{R}(ce_{1}+de_{1}^{\ast})
.
By the basis
\{e_{1},e_{1}^{\ast}\}
, we identity
W
with
\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
X^{\ast}
with
\mathbb{R}
,
X
with
\mathbb{R}
.

\displaystyle\theta_{Y^{\ast},L}(f)(xe_{Y})

\displaystyle=\int_{Y^{\ast}/Y^{\ast}\cap L}f([te_{Y^{\ast}},0]+[xe_{Y},0])dt

(7.61)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}f(xe_{y}+te_{Y^{\ast}})\psi(-\tfrac{xt}{2})dt.

(7.62)

\displaystyle\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})(ye_{1}+ze_{1}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}(ye_{1}+ze_{1}^{\ast}+te_{1})\psi(\tfrac{\langle te_{1},ye_{1}+ze_{1}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})

(7.63)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}(ye_{1}+ze_{1}^{\ast}+te_{1})\psi(\tfrac{tz}{2}).

(7.64)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{Y^{\ast},X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})(xe_{Y})

\displaystyle=\int_{te_{Y^{\ast}}\in Y^{\ast}}f^{\prime}([te_{Y^{\ast}},0]+[xe_{Y},0])|A_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.65)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([te_{Y^{\ast}},0]+[xe_{Y},0])|A_{Y^{\ast}X^{\ast}}|^{1/2}dt

(7.66)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([(td+xb)e_{1}^{\ast},\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-tx}{2}][(tc+xa)e_{1},0])|c|^{1/2}dt

(7.67)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\psi(\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-tx}{2})f^{\prime}([(tc+xa)e_{1},0])|c|^{1/2}dt.

(7.68)

\displaystyle\theta_{Y^{\ast},L}[\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})](xe_{Y})

(7.69)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})(xe_{y}+te_{Y^{\ast}})\psi(-\tfrac{xt}{2})dt

(7.70)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})((xa+tc)e_{1}+(xb+td)e_{2}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{xt}{2})dt

(7.71)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}((xa+tc)e_{1}+(xb+td)e_{1}^{\ast}+se_{1})\psi(\tfrac{s(xb+td)}{2})\psi(-\tfrac{xt}{2})dt

(7.72)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}([(td+xb)e_{1}^{\ast},\tfrac{(tc+xa+s)(td+xb)}{2}][(tc+xa+s)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{s(xb+td)}{2})\psi(-\tfrac{xt}{2})dt

(7.73)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}([(tc+xa+s)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-xt}{2}+s(xb+td))dt

(7.74)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}([(tc+xa+cs+l)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-xt}{2}+(cs+l)(xb+td))dt

(7.75)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}([((t+s)c+xa+l)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{((t+s)c+xa)((t+s)d+xb)-x(t+s)}{2}+l(xb+(t+s)d))dt

(7.76)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}f^{\prime}([(tc+xa+l)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-xt}{2}+l(xb+td))dt

(7.77)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([(tc+xa)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{([t-\tfrac{l}{c}]c+xa)([t-\tfrac{l}{c}]d+xb)-x[t-\tfrac{l}{c}]}{2}+l[t-\tfrac{l}{c}]d+lxb)dt

(7.78)

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}([(tc+xa)e_{1},0])\psi(\tfrac{(tc+xa)(td+xb)-xt}{2})[\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}\psi(-\tfrac{dl^{2}}{2c})]dt.

(7.79)

Hence

\widetilde{\beta}(g)=|c|^{-1/2}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}/c\mathbb{Z}}\psi(-\tfrac{dl^{2}}{2c})=\beta(d,c).

Lemma 7.8
.

The restriction of
[\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}]
on
\Gamma(2)
is trivial, with an explicit trivialization:

\begin{array}[]{rlcl}\overline{s}:&\Gamma(2)&\longrightarrow&\mu_{8};\\
&\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}&\longmapsto&\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i[1-\operatorname{sgn}(d)]}{4}}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
\beta(d,c)e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

such that
\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\overline{\beta}(g_{1})^{-1}\overline{\beta}(g_{2})^{-1}\overline{\beta}(g_{1}g_{2})
, for
g_{i}\in\Gamma(2)
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})\widetilde{c}_{{X^{\ast}}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=[\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})^{-1}][\widetilde{\beta}(g_{1})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})^{-1}]^{-1}[\widetilde{\beta}(g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})^{-1}]^{-1}.

So we can take
\overline{\beta}(g)=\widetilde{\beta}(g)m_{X^{\ast}}(g)^{-1}
.

1) If
c=0
,
\widetilde{\beta}(g)=1
,
m_{X^{\ast}}(g)^{-1}=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i[1-\operatorname{sgn}(d)]}{4}}
.

2) If
c\neq 0
,
\widetilde{\beta}(g)=\beta(d,c)
,
m_{X^{\ast}}(g)^{-1}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i\operatorname{sgn}(c)}{4}}
.
∎

Note that
\Gamma^{\pm}(2)\cap\operatorname{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R})=\langle\pm I\rangle
. By calculation, one can see that the two trivialization maps on
\langle\pm I\rangle
are different, up to a sign character. As
\Gamma(2)\simeq\langle\pm I\rangle\times\langle n_{2},n^{-}_{2}\rangle
, for
n_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
,
n^{-}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix}
, let us modify the trivialization map on
\Gamma(2)
by the sign character and write it by
\overline{\beta}^{-}
. Similarly, we can modify the trivialization map
\widetilde{\beta}
on
\Gamma(2)
by the sign character and write it by
\widetilde{\beta}^{-}
.

7.3.
The splitting of
\widehat{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbb{Z})

In this section, we will let

g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix},h=\begin{pmatrix}p&q\\
r&s\end{pmatrix}
be two elements of
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let us first recall some results of Dedekind sums from
[
RaWh
]
,
[
Po
]
.

•

((x))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}x-[x]-\tfrac{1}{2}&\textrm{ if }x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{Z},\\
0&\textrm{ if }x\in\mathbb{Z}.\end{array}\right.

•

s(d,c)=\sum_{k\bmod(|c|)}((\tfrac{k}{c}))((\tfrac{kd}{c}))
, for two coprime integers
c,d
.(Dedekind sum)

•

s(-d,c)=-s(d,c)
,
s(d,-c)=s(d,c)
.

Theorem 7.9
.

(a)

For positive odd
d
, we have

12ds(c,d)\equiv d+1-(\tfrac{c}{d})(\bmod 8),

where
(\tfrac{c}{d})
is the Jacobi symbol.

(b)

If
c>0,d>0
and
c,d
are coprime, we have

12s(d,c)+12s(c,d)=-3+\tfrac{d}{c}+\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{1}{cd}.

Proof.

See
[
RaWh
, Theorems 3, 18]
.
∎

Theorem 7.10
.

s(c,d)+s(r,s)=s(cu-dv,cs+dr)-\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{1}{12}(\tfrac{d}{st}+\tfrac{s}{td}+\tfrac{t}{ds})
, for all positive
c,d,r,s
, where
t=cs+dr
and
u,v
are any two integers such that
ru+sv=1
.

Proof.

See
[
Po
, Theorem 7]
.
∎

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. In
[
As
]
, Asai gave the following function:

\nu(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tfrac{b}{12d}+\tfrac{1-sgn(d)}{4}&\textrm{ if }c=0,\\
\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)\bigg(\tfrac{1}{4}+s(d,|c|)\bigg)&\textrm{ if }c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

By
[
As
]
, the signature cocycle on
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with values on
\mathbb{Z}
can be splitting by the above function. In fact, the signature cocycle differs from the Rao-Perrin cocycle only by a character
e^{-\pi i(-)}
. So let us define:

•

\overline{\beta_{1}}(g)=e^{-\pi i\nu(g)}
.

•

\widetilde{\beta_{1}}(g_{i})=\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b}{12a}}&c=0,\\
e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}&c\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Let
g_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{i}&b_{i}\\
c_{i}&d_{i}\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Lemma 7.11
.

\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})^{-1}\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{2})^{-1}\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{1}g_{2})
.

Proof.

See
[
As
, 2-3]
.
∎

Lemma 7.12
.

\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\widetilde{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta_{1}}(g_{2})^{-1}\widetilde{\beta_{1}}(g_{1}g_{2})
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{{X^{\ast}}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})^{-1}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=[\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})][\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{1})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})]^{-1}[\overline{\beta_{1}}(g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})]^{-1}.

∎

Now let us consider the subgroup
\Gamma(2)\subseteq\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. According to Lemma
7.5
, the cocycle
\widetilde{c}_{{X^{\ast}}}
on
\Gamma(2)
can also be splitting by the function
\widetilde{\beta}
. So
\widetilde{\beta}
differs from
\widetilde{\beta_{1}}
by a character
\chi
. Let us formulate this character explicitly.
Let
\chi(g)=\widetilde{\beta_{1}}(g)/\widetilde{\beta}(g)
, for
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
.

•

\displaystyle 12\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sgn}(d)12\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(|d|,|c|)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sgn}(d)[-3\operatorname{sgn}(c)+\tfrac{|d|}{c}+\tfrac{c}{|d|}+\tfrac{1}{c|d|}-12s(c,|d|)]

\displaystyle=-3\operatorname{sgn}(cd)+\tfrac{d}{c}+\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{1}{cd}-12\operatorname{sgn}(d)s(c,d).

\displaystyle\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{12}[\tfrac{a}{c}+\tfrac{d}{c}-12\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{12c}[a+d+3c\operatorname{sgn}(cd)-d-\tfrac{c^{2}}{d}-\tfrac{1}{d}+12\operatorname{sgn}(d)cs(c,d)]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{12}[3\operatorname{sgn}(cd)-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12\operatorname{sgn}(d)s(c,d)].

•

If
d>0
,
c=2c^{\prime}
, then

\betaup(d,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4),\\
(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

•

If
d<0
,
c=2c^{\prime}
, then

\betaup(d,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(cd)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 3(\bmod 4),\\
(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})(-i)e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(cd)}&\textrm{ if }d\equiv 1(\bmod 4).\end{array}\right.

•

If
c=0
,
\chi(g)=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b}{12a}}
.

•

If
c\neq 0
,
d>0
,
d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
, and
c=2c^{\prime}
, then:

\displaystyle\chi(g)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c,d)]}.

•

If
c\neq 0
,
d>0
,
d\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
, and
c=2c^{\prime}
, then:

\displaystyle\chi(g)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})(-i)e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c,d)]}.

•

If
c\neq 0
,
d<0
,
d\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
, and
c=2c^{\prime}
, then:

\displaystyle\chi(g)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{1}{12}[-3\operatorname{sgn}(c)-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}-12s(c,d)]}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})e^{-\pi i\tfrac{1}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}-12s(c,d)]}.

•

If
c\neq 0
,
d<0
,
d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
, and
c=2c^{\prime}
, then:

\displaystyle\chi(g)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{1}{12}[-3\operatorname{sgn}(c)-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}-12s(c,d)]}(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})ie^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})ie^{-\pi i\tfrac{1}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}-12s(c,d)]}.

Let
n_{2k}=\begin{pmatrix}1&2k\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
,
n^{-}_{2k}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2k&1\end{pmatrix}
. Note that
\chi(n_{2k})=e^{-\tfrac{k\pi i}{6}}
,
\chi(n^{-}_{2k})=e^{\tfrac{k\pi i}{6}}
.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma(2)
, and
c=2c^{\prime}
.

(1)

\chi(\pm g)=\chi(g)
.

(2)

\chi(gn_{2})=\chi(g)\chi(n_{2})

(3)

\chi(gn^{-}_{2})=\chi(g)\chi(n^{-}_{2})
.

Proof.

1) If
c=0
,
\chi(-g)=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{-b}{-12a}}=\chi(g)
.

If
c\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\chi(-g)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{-a-d}{-12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(-c)s(-d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(-d,-c)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(-d,-c)

\displaystyle=\chi(g)\beta^{-1}(-d,-c)/\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=\chi(g).

2) Note that
gn_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b+2a\\
c&d+2c\end{pmatrix}
.

If
c=0
,
\chi(gn_{2})=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b+2a}{12a}}=\chi(g)\tfrac{-2\pi i}{12}=\chi(g)\chi(n_{2})
.

If
c\neq 0
, then:

\displaystyle\chi(gn_{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d+2c}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d+2c,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d+2c,c)

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{1}{6}}e^{-\pi i\Big[\tfrac{a+d}{12c}-\operatorname{sgn}(c)s(d,|c|)\Big]}\beta^{-1}(d,c)

\displaystyle=\chi(n_{2})\chi(g).

3) Note that
gn^{-}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a+2b&b\\
c+2d&d\end{pmatrix}
.

If
c=0
,
gn^{-}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
2d&d\end{pmatrix}
. Then

\displaystyle\chi(gn^{-}_{2})

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[3\operatorname{sgn}(2dd)-\tfrac{2d}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12\operatorname{sgn}(d)s(2d,d)]}\beta^{-1}(d,2d)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[3-2+\tfrac{b}{d}]}\beta^{-1}(1,2)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}\tfrac{b}{d}}e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}\tfrac{b}{d}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}

\displaystyle=\chi(g)\chi(n^{-}_{2}).

If
c\neq 0
, by Part (1), we can assume
d>0
.

a)
c+2d=0
,
d=1
,
c=-2
,
a+2b=1
. Then:

\chi(gn^{-}_{2})=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b}{12}},\quad\quad\chi(n^{-}_{2})=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{6}};

\displaystyle\chi(g)

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}\tfrac{1}{12}[3\operatorname{sgn}(cd)-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12\operatorname{sgn}(d)s(c,d)]}\betaup(d,c)^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}\tfrac{1}{12}[-3+2+b]}\betaup(1,-2)^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b}{12}}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{12}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\pi i\tfrac{b}{12}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}

\displaystyle=\chi(gn^{-}_{2})\chi(n^{-}_{2})^{-1}.

b)
c+2d\neq 0
,
d\equiv 1(\bmod 4)
,
c=2c^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\chi(gn_{2}^{-})

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}+d}{d})e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c+2d}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c+2d,d)]}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{d})e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c,d)]}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}

\displaystyle=\chi(g)\chi(n_{2}^{-}).

c)
c+2d\neq 0
,
d\equiv 3(\bmod 4)
,
c=2c^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\chi(gn_{2}^{-})

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}+d}{d})(-i)e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c+2d}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c+2d,d)]}

\displaystyle=(\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{|d|})(-i)e^{\tfrac{-\pi i}{12}[-\tfrac{c}{d}+\tfrac{b}{d}+12s(c,d)]}e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}

\displaystyle=\chi(g)\chi(n_{2}^{-}).

∎

According to A. Putman’s answer on the question titled ’Generators for congruence group
\Gamma(2)
’ on MathOverflow,
\Gamma(2)/\{\pm I\}
is a free group of two generators
n_{2},n_{2}^{-}
. So by the above lemma,
\chi
is a character of
\Gamma(2)
. Moreover, this character is determined by
\chi(\pm I)=1
,
\chi(n_{2})=e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}
,
\chi(n_{2}^{-})=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{6}}
.

Question 7.14
.

•

Does everything can be done for higher rank symplectic groups?

•

Moreover, one can also connect them with A. Putman, J.E. Pommersheim, R.Kirby, P.Melvin R.Brooks, or others’ works in some sense?

7.4.
Two models

Go back to the formulas (
7.59
)(
7.60
). Take
Y^{\ast}=X^{\ast},Y=X
. Through the isomorphisms
\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}
,
\theta_{X^{\ast},L}
, we can transfer the actions of
\widehat{\operatorname{Sp}}(W)
from
\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})
to
\mathcal{H}(L)
. For an element
f\in\mathcal{H}(L)
, let
f^{\prime}=\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)\in\mathcal{H}(X^{\ast})
. Then

f=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}(f^{\prime})
. For
g\in\widehat{\operatorname{Sp}}(W)
, we can define

\pi_{\psi}(g)f=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})]=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g)\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)].

(7.80)

Under such action, for
g_{1},g_{2}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Sp}}(W)
,

\begin{split}\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})[\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})f]&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}]([\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})f])\\
&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)]))\\
&=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}[\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})\theta_{X^{\ast},L}(f)]\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})\pi_{\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})f.\end{split}

(7.81)

7.5.
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

In particular, let us consider
g\in\operatorname{Sp}(L)
. Under the basis
\{e_{1},e_{1}^{\ast}\}
,
\operatorname{Sp}(L)\simeq\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let
w=x+x^{\ast}\in W
.

Case 1:
g=u(b)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
2\mid b
.

\begin{split}\pi_{\psi}(g)f(w)&=\sum_{l\in L/L\cap X^{\ast}}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(w+l)\psi(\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2}+\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(w+l)\psi(\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(x+l)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle(x+l),(x+l)b\rangle)f^{\prime}(x+l)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}\psi(\tfrac{1}{2}\langle(x+l),(x+l)b\rangle)f(x+l+\dot{y}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x+l,\dot{y}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(x+l+\dot{y}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x+l,\dot{y}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,x^{\ast}+xb\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}+xb\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=f(wg).\end{split}

Case 2:
g=h(a)\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
,
a\in\{\pm 1\}
.

\begin{split}\pi_{\psi}(g)f(w)&=\sum_{l\in L/L\cap X^{\ast}}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(w+l)\psi(\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2}+\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(x+l)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}f^{\prime}(-x-l)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(-x-l+\dot{y}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle-x-l,\dot{y}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(-x+l+\dot{y}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle-x+l,\dot{y}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,-w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle-x,-x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})d\dot{y}^{\ast}\\
&=f(wg).\end{split}

(7.82)

Case 3:
g=\omega\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
.

\begin{split}\pi_{\psi}(g)f(w)&=\sum_{l\in L/L\cap X^{\ast}}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(w+l)\psi(\tfrac{\langle l,w\rangle}{2}+\tfrac{\langle x_{l},x^{\ast}_{l}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\pi_{\psi}(g)(f^{\prime})(x+l)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}f^{\prime}(y^{\ast}\omega^{-1})\psi(\langle x+l,y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dy^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}f^{\prime}(y^{\ast}\omega^{-1})\psi(\langle x,y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\langle l,x^{\ast}+y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dy^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(y^{\ast}\omega^{-1}+\dot{z}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle y^{\ast}\omega^{-1},\dot{z}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle x+l,y^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\langle l,w\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dz^{\ast}dy^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l^{\ast}\in L\cap X^{\ast}}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f((-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast})\omega^{-1}+\dot{z}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle(-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast})\omega^{-1},\dot{z}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle x,-x^{\ast}+l^{\ast}\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dz^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f((-x^{\ast}\omega^{-1}+l+\dot{z}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle(-x^{\ast})\omega^{-1}+l,\dot{z}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle x,-x^{\ast}+l\omega\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dz^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(x^{\ast}\omega+l+\dot{z}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x^{\ast}\omega+l,\dot{z}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,x\omega\rangle)\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x,x^{\ast}\rangle}{2})dz^{\ast}\\
&=\sum_{l\in L\cap X}\int_{X^{\ast}/X^{\ast}\cap L}f(x^{\ast}\omega+l+\dot{z}^{\ast})\psi(-\tfrac{\langle x^{\ast}\omega+l,\dot{z}^{\ast}\rangle}{2})\psi(\langle l,x\omega\rangle)\psi(\tfrac{\langle x^{\ast}\omega,x\omega\rangle}{2})dz^{\ast}\\
&=f(wg).\end{split}

(7.83)

Case 4:
g=u_{-}(c)=\omega^{-1}u(-c)\omega\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
2\mid c
. Then:

\begin{split}\pi_{\psi}(g)f(w)&=\pi_{\psi}(\omega^{-1}u(-c)\omega)f(w)\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}\pi_{\psi}(\omega^{-1})\pi_{\psi}(u(-c))\pi_{\psi}(\omega)f(w)\\
&=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\omega^{-1}u(-c),\omega)^{-1}\pi_{\psi}(\omega)f(wg)\\
&=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(c)}f(wg).\end{split}

(7.84)

Recall
n_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&2\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
,
n^{-}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
2&1\end{pmatrix}
. Moreover,
\Gamma(2)/{\pm I}
is a free group with the generators
n_{2}
,
n^{-}_{2}
. For any
g\in\Gamma(2)
, let us write it in the form

\displaystyle g=(-I)^{\epsilon}n_{2}^{k_{1}}(n^{-}_{2})^{k_{2}}\cdots n_{2}^{k_{l}}(n^{-}_{2})^{k_{l+1}}.

(7.85)

Hence for any
g
, there exists
\epsilon_{g}\in\mu_{8}
, which is a product of Weil indexes such that

\pi_{\psi}(g)f(w)=\epsilon_{g}f(wg).

(7.86)

Note that
\epsilon_{g}
is determined by
(\ref{eq5})
, and can be calculated from (
7.85
). Then: for
g_{1},g_{2}\in\Gamma(2)
, we have:

\displaystyle\pi_{\psi}(g_{1})\pi_{\psi}(g_{2})f(w)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{g_{1}}\epsilon_{g_{2}}f(wg_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})\pi_{\psi}(g_{1}g_{2})f(w)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})\epsilon_{g_{1}g_{2}}f(wg_{1}g_{2}).

Hence

\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\epsilon_{g_{1}}\epsilon_{g_{2}}\epsilon_{g_{1}g_{2}}^{-1}.

According to Lemma
7.5
,
\widetilde{\beta}
differs from
\epsilon_{g}^{-1}
by a character
\chiup
, where
\chiup(g)=\widetilde{\beta}(g)\epsilon_{g}
.

1) If
g=-I
,
\chiup(g)=1
.

2) If
g=n_{2}
,
\chiup(g)=1
.

3) If
g=n_{2}^{-}
,
\chiup(g)=\widetilde{\beta}(g)\epsilon_{g}=\beta(1,2)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}\operatorname{sgn}(2)}=1
.
Hence
\widetilde{\beta}(g)=\epsilon_{g}^{-1}
.
